Rationale and the rejected options: `docs/followups/2026-08-10-s8b-nil-server-version-decision.md`. **The string `getServerVersion()` returns is NORMALISED (M2-C5b, §22.2 (bz))**: on PostgreSQL the leading product name is stripped, so the string begins with the version number as `pdo_pgsql`'s does (Ferro's keeps the banner's `on …, compiled by …` tail, which `pdo_pgsql` omits), because callers compare it with `version_compare()`, which reads `PostgreSQL 17.10 (…)` as OLDER than every version; on the MySQL family it is returned byte-identical, because `-MariaDB` is what selects the dialect.
- **`setTransactionIsolation()` requires `wrapperClass`.** Doctrine's implementation emits `SET SESSION …`, one of the two forms §22.2 (s) names as forbidden on a transaction-mode pool; without the wrapper the driver REFUSES the statement loudly rather than let it report success and change nothing. See §22.2 (ad).
- DBAL middlewares (logging, schema managers, migrations) operate above the driver SPI and work unchanged. The stock **PostgreSQL** schema manager — blocked at the M1-S8b acceptance gate by `pg_index.indkey`, an `int2vector` — works since M1-S9 (§22.2 (ae)), so `doctrine/migrations` and schema diffing work on every family; `doctrine/migrations` runs its DDL through the same session (DDL pins per §7). The isolation `wrapperClass` finds its driver connection through `getNativeConnection()`, which every middleware base class forwards, so it works behind a driver middleware — which DoctrineBundle installs on every connection by default (§22.2 (by)).
- Known incompatibilities to document: bundles calling `getNativeConnection()` expecting PDO; `pdo_pgsql`-specific COPY hacks (replaced by a first-class `Ferro\Pg\Copy` API, which does not exist yet); persistent-connection tuning advice (obsolete under Ferro); **anything shelling out to `pg_dump`/`mysqldump` with application config credentials — the credentials no longer exist in PHP** (D8: ops provisions separate dump credentials; no engine passthrough in v1); and **Doctrine ORM on PostgreSQL under the default IDENTITY strategy**, which cannot insert through Ferro because `IdentityGenerator::generateId()` is `(int) $conn->lastInsertId()` — the remedy is the ORM's SEQUENCE identity strategy, configured, and it means **drop-in is config-only for DBAL and explicitly NOT config-only for ORM on PostgreSQL** (D-S8b-5). *[Superseded M2 (§22.2 (ci)): that last item is WITHDRAWN. `lastInsertId()` now answers inside a transaction with `lastval()` on the pinned connection, the unit of work always inserts in one, and ORM identity generation on PostgreSQL is config-only. Only `lastInsertId()` OUTSIDE a transaction still throws on PostgreSQL.]* The incompat list is a first-class doc page with per-package workarounds: `docs/known-incompatibilities.md`, begun at M1-S8b and carrying both tiers' per-package catalogue since M2 (§22.2 (bq)).
- **Acceptance — RESTATED, because the v0.1 bar is not reachable and the gap is recorded rather than narrowed away (§22.2 (z)).** The original clause read "DBAL 4 functional test suite green on PG + MySQL + SQLite; ORM functional suite green on PG + MySQL". **SQLite is impossible in M1** (`AnyPool` is `{ Pg | Mysql }`), **the ORM suite is not run** (`doctrine/orm` is not a dependency of this repository and its harness has the same driver-injection problem), **the upstream suite cannot select a third-party `driverClass` without a patched `TestUtil`** (it silently falls back to in-memory SQLite and passes against the wrong engine — measured), and **the stock drivers are not themselves green on the project's containers**. The bar is therefore: **the curated subset in `testkit/dbal/allowlist.txt`, run through the real `DriverManager` against a real backend with driver identity asserted before the first test, green modulo a triaged and recorded list.** Runner: `testkit/dbal-suite.sh` (§20.3). Recorded numbers, environment manifest, reproducibility evidence and the per-test triage: `docs/dbal-suite/2026-08-11-results.md`.

*[Amended M2 (§22.2 (ci)): **the ORM functional suite IS run now.** `testkit/orm-suite.sh` runs upstream doctrine/orm 3.7.3's whole functional directory (1627 tests, upstream CI's group exclusions) through `Ferro\DBAL\Driver` on DBAL 4.4.4, with a contact-asserted stock-PDO control per column, compared as sets including skips. Local first measurement: PostgreSQL under the **stock** ORM configuration 1571/1597 against a control at 1597/1597 (397 before this slice's `lastInsertId()` change), MariaDB 10.11 1579/1586 against 1586/1586, the skip sets equal to the controls'; every Ferro-only non-pass is a documented incompatibility (sub-second `TIMESTAMPTZ` refusal, the §7.4 TEMP-table class, `lastInsertId()` outside a transaction, and a NULL-typed select list on the MySQL family). The bar as written ("green") is NOT met. MySQL 8.4 is measured only by the on-demand `orm-suite` workflow (RECORDED: `docs/orm-suite/2026-10-02-ci-results.md`). **The DBAL 3 lane of the ORM suite is not run anywhere**: the workflow has no such lane.]*

## 15. Eloquent tier (`ferro/laravel`) — drop-in

**Goal:** `config/database.php` changes `driver` and nothing else. Models, query builder, migrations, seeds, `DB::` facade, pagination, `chunk`/`lazy`, upserts, transactions with `attempts`, and events all behave identically.

```php
'connections' => [
    'mysql' => [
        'driver'       => 'ferro-mysql',          // was 'mysql'
        'ferro_socket' => '/run/ferro/app.sock',  // the ferrod socket (or ferro_host/ferro_port)
        'pool'         => 'main',
        'database'     => 'app',                  // REQUIRED by Illuminate — see below
        // host/username/password removed — credentials live in ferrod (§12)
    ],
],
```

*[Amended M2-C1f: the example had no `ferro_socket`, which the tier refuses to start without, and carried `'read' => ['pool' => 'main_ro']`, which is not implemented and which Illuminate's `ConnectionFactory` cannot even parse without a matching `write` key (a `TypeError` in `mergeReadWriteConfig()`, measured). It also called `database` "a LABEL here, not a selector" — true on PostgreSQL and SQLite, where the pool's DSN chooses the database, and FALSE on MySQL/MariaDB, where `MySqlBuilder` passes it into every `information_schema` query; there it must name the pool's database, and the tier refuses a mismatch (§22.2 (cb)).]*

**Mechanism.** A service provider registers `ferro-{mysql,mariadb,pgsql,sqlite,sqlsrv}` via `Illuminate\Database\Connection::resolverFor()` (as of M2: `ferro-pgsql`, `ferro-sqlite`, `ferro-mysql` and `ferro-mariadb`, the last two since C1f — one per Laravel family, and Laravel 11 resolves MariaDB through its own `mariadb` driver; `ferro-sqlsrv` waits on the SQL Server backend, §8). Each resolver builds `Ferro{MySql,MariaDb,Postgres,SQLite,SqlServer}Connection extends` the corresponding Illuminate connection class — inheriting the stock Grammar and Processor, which only build SQL strings and post-process results and therefore need no changes. The subclass overrides the execution layer:

- `select()`, `selectResultSets()`, `cursor()` → EXEC frames; `cursor()` maps to the credit-based stream (§5.2) surfaced as `LazyCollection` — `Model::lazy()`/`chunkById()` get constant-memory behavior for free.
- `statement()`, `affectingStatement()`, `unprepared()` → EXEC with `fetch: none`, `affected` returned.
- `beginTransaction()/commit()/rollBack()` and savepoint compilation → TX service; `DB::transaction($fn, attempts: 3)` maps `attempts` to the engine's retryable classes (deadlock/serialization) so retries actually re-run the closure on a fresh tx.
- `getPdo()/getReadPdo()` return a **`FerroPdoShim`** — a thin PDO-interface adapter implementing only the residual surface the framework and common packages touch (`quote`, `lastInsertId`, `inTransaction`, `exec`, `getAttribute(SERVER_VERSION | DRIVER_NAME)` — the second since the demo app below measured the `database` queue needing it, §22.2 (ch)). It exists so `ManagesTransactions` internals and ecosystem packages keep working. **The shim OWNS the connection's Ferro client**, and every execution path reaches the client through `getPdo()`, so Illuminate's reconnect — which replaces the PDO and never the connection — moves statements and transactions together, as PDO's does (§22.2 (bw)). *[Amended M2-C1e-2: this bullet originally said `getRawPdo()` "throws with a pointer to this section". It must not: Illuminate's own `DatabaseManager` reconnector reads `getRawPdo()` on every `DB::reconnect()`, on `run()`'s reconnect after `DB::disconnect()`, and on the lost-connection retry, so a throwing override would break reconnection outright. It is not overridden; it returns the shim, or the unresolved closure that DIALS a new client when resolved — once per resolution, as the PDO closure `ConnectionFactory` hands a stock connection does. Illuminate passes that closure between connection objects, and a closure that captured an already-dialled client let two connections share one session (§22.2 (bw)).]* *(A `read`/`write` split — which an earlier version of the config example above showed — is NOT implemented by this tier: every statement runs on the connection's one pool, and with a `read` key `getReadPdo()` is Illuminate's stock connector closure, which throws `Unsupported driver`. Explicit replica routing is §7.5, M4.)*
- `lastInsertId` follows each family's PDO driver, because the drivers disagree and Illuminate inherits their contract: per STATEMENT on MySQL/MariaDB, `"0"` when it generated none, as `pdo_mysql` (§22.2 (cb)); sticky on the HANDLE on SQLite, as `pdo_sqlite` (§22.2 (bn)); PostgreSQL uses `insert … returning`. `Schema` builder and migrations run through `statement()` unchanged, with the stock builders — except where a schema operation depends on SESSION state across statements, which a transaction-mode pool does not carry (§7.4): `FerroSQLiteBuilder::dropAllTables()` (§22.2 (bn)) and the MySQL family's foreign-key-check pinning (§22.2 (cb)) run their stock-grammar statements on one pinned connection. *[Amended M2-C1f: this bullet said "`lastInsertId` semantics identical to §14", which neither family's tier is, and that the schema builder ran unchanged.]*
- **Known incompatibilities to document:** `php artisan schema:dump` and `php artisan db` (both spawn native clients with config credentials, which no longer exist — D8), `spatie/laravel-backup` and similar dump-based packages, packages reaching for `PDO::pgsqlCopyFromArray` (Ferro-native `Ferro\Pg\Copy` equivalent documented). Same incompat doc page as §14.

**Acceptance:** the `illuminate/database` integration test suite green on MySQL, PG, SQLite via Ferro connections; a Laravel demo app (auth scaffolding + queues + Horizon DB metrics) runs with only the config diff above. Octane supported in sync mode (§10.1 caveat).

*[Amended M2 (§22.2 (ch)): **Horizon has no database workload to demonstrate.** Every Horizon repository is Redis (`RedisJobRepository`, `RedisMetricsRepository`, `RedisTagRepository`, `RedisWorkloadRepository`, the supervisor and process repositories) — its metrics live in Redis, not in a database — and its database reads are the batches screen's: `DatabaseBatchRepository::get()`/`find()` and, since Horizon 5.45, a direct `like` search over `job_batches` on the batching connection. The demo app (`testkit/laravel-demo/`) therefore demonstrates what a Laravel application actually puts in its database: auth with the `database` session driver (register, login, logout, an authenticated route), password-reset tokens, the `database` queue (jobs, a failed job and `queue:retry`, a `Bus::batch` with its `then` callback, and the three batch reads Horizon's screen makes), and the `database` cache store with atomic locks. "Only the config diff" holds in two halves: no PHP to REGISTER the driver — `ferro/laravel` ships `FerroServiceProvider` under `extra.laravel.providers`, found by Laravel's own `PackageManifest` (until the demo's review an application had to call `FerroConnections::register()` itself, which is code); and no PHP to RUN the app — one test file runs under every column, the Ferro column and the stock-PDO control differing only in the `database.connections` entry. The stock-name ALIAS stays an explicit `register()` call, because it replaces every connection using that name. Measured locally on PostgreSQL 16, MariaDB 10.11 (under both Laravel families) and SQLite, Ferro and control alike; a per-PR CI gate (`ci.yml`'s `laravel-demo`) runs it on PostgreSQL 17, MySQL 8.4, MariaDB 11.8 and SQLite.]*

## 16. Performance targets (v1 exit criteria)

- Boundary overhead (client call → engine → trivial `SELECT 1` → response): **p50 < 60 µs, p99 < 200 µs** on loopback UDS, vs local PDO baseline.
- Equal-throughput upstream connection count reduced **≥ 5×** on the reference workload (200 FPM workers, pgbench-like mix).
- 10-query fan-out latency ≤ max(single query) + 2 ms under Fibers.
  *[Amended M3-D1e (§22.2 (cs)): MET on the D17 runner in both recorded runs (p99 over a single query +0.53 to +1.30 ms, 95% upper bound ≤ 1.65 ms). Measured by `ferro-bench --scenario fanout` (`bench.yml` on the D17 runner). "max(single query)" is read CONSERVATIVELY as the latency distribution of ONE of the k identical statements (the literal max over k draws is larger, so this can only turn a MET into a MISSED); the target is judged at p50 and p99, each `fanout − single` carrying a 95% bootstrap interval, and is MET at a percentile only when the interval's upper bound is within 2 ms — in BOTH of D17's two runs. Overlap is proven per iteration: every fan-out must be answered by k distinct backend sessions.]*
- Streaming a 1 GB result: client RSS bounded by window (≤ 16 MiB), engine RSS bounded by per-stream buffers.
- Statement-cache hit rate > 95 % steady-state on the reference app (made achievable by conditional hygiene, §7.2). *[Unmeasurable until §7.3's cache exists — §13, §22.2 (bv).]*

All targets are measured against a **recorded reference environment** — `bench/README` pins CPU model, kernel, PHP version, and JIT status; numbers are meaningless otherwise.

### 16.1 Boundary budget and the M0 decision gate

Component budget for the trivial-call path, pure-PHP client:

| component | est. µs |
|---|---|
| 2× UDS syscalls (write/read) | 2–4 |
| MessagePack encode+decode, pure PHP | 5–15 (JIT-dependent) |
| frame header pack/unpack | ~0.5 |
| engine hop (decode, pool checkout hit, dispatch) | 5–10 |
| backend `SELECT 1` round trip | 20–40 |
| hydration (memoized plan) | ~1/row |

p50 < 60 µs is reachable pure-PHP. **p99 is where pure PHP dies first** — GC pauses, JIT-less hosts, wide rows. Mitigations in order: runtime-detect `ext-msgpack` (`composer suggest`, transparent swap); header codec is a single `pack`/`unpack` either way. **Decision gate (D12): the M0 bench harness measures p99 on the reference environment with the pure-PHP codec. A miss pulls the `ext-php-rs` accelerator (frame codec + hydration, same wire contract) forward into M1.** M0 does not exit without this measurement recorded.

## 17. Delivery milestones

- **M0** — `/proto` registry + golden vectors; frame codec (Rust) + fuzz target; `ferrod` core (session layer, HELLO/epoch, PING/PONG); PG pool (hand-rolled, D9); EXEC/TX happy paths; sync PHP client with codec autodetect and reconnect-loop skeleton; bench harness + PDO baseline. **Exit gate: D12 measurement recorded.**
- **M1** — pin engine as specified (§7.1–7.2: protocol signals, trackers, assist lexer, conditional hygiene) + MySQL backend + tracker verification test; full error taxonomy incl. `Indeterminate`; Doctrine driver; DBAL 4 suite green. Accelerator work lands here iff D12 gate failed.
- **M2** — Eloquent tier + PDO shim; Illuminate suite green; observability (OTLP/Prometheus/slow log); SQLite engine-owned mode; DBAL `^3.8` bridge; incompat doc page (§14–15).
- **M3** — Fibers multiplexing; `ferro check`/`gen` with `idempotent` manifest; manifest handshake; memfd path behind `MEMFD_RX`; COPY API.
- **M4** — MSSQL (mode per D1 outcome); manifest-only hardening mode; replica routing + lag gating; `ferro top`.
- **M5** — LISTEN/NOTIFY streams; Octane guidance; packaging (deb/rpm/container sidecar; systemd templated + socket-activated units per §18).
- **M6 — Ferro HTTP** (D16): its SPEC section and service id first, then the engine (named upstreams, pools, TLS, fate taxonomy, per-upstream breakers and rate limits, streaming bodies), the PHP Guzzle handler and PSR-18 client, and a drop-in acceptance bar with a stock-handler control.
- **M7 — Ferro Queue** (D16): its SPEC section and service id first, then the engine (leases, acks, retries, delayed delivery, transactional enqueue on `tx_id`) over PG `SKIP LOCKED`, the Laravel Queue and Symfony Messenger transports, and a drop-in acceptance bar with a stock-driver control.
- **v1 exit** — every milestone above complete; each suite bar green per D18; the chaos suite green on every shipped backend and engine; §16 measured and recorded per D17.

*[Amended 2026-10-06 (D16): M6 and M7 are added to v1. **Build order is M3 → M6 → M7 → M4 → M5 → v1 exit.** The engines come before M4/M5 because they depend on M3's Fibers multiplexing (fan-out across one socket), its manifest (`idempotent` is how a retry is licensed, which HTTP's `Idempotency-Key` mirrors) and memfd (bulk bodies), while nothing in M4/M5 depends on them. Packaging stays last, so it packages what v1 actually ships.]*

*[Amended 2026-10-06 (§23, §24; decided under the owner's full-freedom grant): **M6's slice plan is §23.15 and M7's is §24.14.** Ledger item D1c (M3: per-request client deadlines with PING liveness, specified in §23.11.0) is a hard prerequisite of HTTP's client slice F8, because today any request slower than the client's 5 s socket timeout poisons its whole session. M6's acceptance bar is §23.12's Lane B plus Lane C; it ships a synchronous PSR-18 client and no Symfony `HttpClientInterface`. **The Symfony Messenger transport leaves v1** (§24.17 Q4): M7's drop-in tier and acceptance bar are Laravel's queue. In the M7 bullet, "retries" means redelivery as stored state (§24.2 I4): the engine never re-runs a job. M7's version gate is PostgreSQL ≥ 12, MySQL ≥ 8.0.1 and MariaDB ≥ 10.6. **No M7 code (G1 onward) starts before D22 is ratified by the owner.**]*

### 17.1 M0 task order

1. `/proto` registry files (`methods.toml`, `errors.toml`, `types.toml`) + constant generation: Rust via `build.rs` in `ferro-proto`, PHP via a small generator script committed alongside.
2. Frame codec in `ferro-proto` + golden vectors in `/proto/vectors/` + `cargo-fuzz` target on the decoder.
3. `ferrod` skeleton: UDS listener, per-session task, HELLO/HELLO_ACK with `boot_epoch`, PING/PONG, GOODBYE, protocol-error frames.
4. Hand-rolled PG pool on `tokio-postgres`: checkout/release, `max_lifetime`, liveness ping. Pin state machine stubbed (everything pins on `BEGIN` via ReadyForQuery only — full engine is M1).
5. SQL service EXEC: param bind, row framing, `stats`, per-request credit windows.
6. TX service: BEGIN/COMMIT/ROLLBACK/savepoints, `tx_id` pinning, deadline timers.
7. PHP client (sync): socket + codec (pure PHP, `ext-msgpack` autodetect), `query/queryOne/rows/scalar`, transaction closure, error mapping v0, reconnect loop with epoch check.
8. Bench harness: trivial-call latency distribution (Ferro vs local PDO), fan-out placeholder, results committed under `bench/results/` with environment manifest.
9. CI: `cargo test/clippy/fmt` (warnings denied), fuzz smoke job, PHPUnit + PHPStan, docker-compose PG service.

## 18. Deployment & lifecycle

Single static binary. **systemd (primary):** templated units — `ferrod@.service` + `ferrod@.socket`, instance name = schema hash, socket unit binds `/run/ferro/%i.sock`. Socket activation means **systemd holds the listener fd across daemon restarts**: connections arriving mid-restart queue in the backlog instead of getting `ECONNREFUSED`, which is what makes §19's reconnect story quiet in practice. Deploys: start `ferrod@{new_hash}.socket`, atomically switch a `current` symlink for tooling, old instance drains via idle TTL and exits; old and new coexist during rollout. (v0.1's single static unit could not express this — hash-versioned paths require the template form.)

Also: pod sidecar sharing an `emptyDir` socket volume. Config `ferro.toml` (pools, DSNs via env/secret refs, policies); `SIGHUP` reload, `SIGTERM` drain. Client discovers the socket by schema-hash path, then `FERRO_ADDR` fallback. Kubernetes readiness = admin `/healthz` (all pools connectable, or degraded-but-serving flag).

*[Amended 2026-10-06 (§23.6.1, §24.3, §24.8): **`ferrod` implements no `SIGHUP` reload in v1**, so every configuration change — pools, upstreams, attached credentials, root certificates, queue stores — takes a restart, and a queue table's shape is re-verified at the next restart. Each restart drains HTTP for up to `FERRO_HTTP_DRAIN_MS` (30 s by default) and then allows `drain_deadline` for the classified terminals to leave, so the unit's `TimeoutStopSec` must exceed `FERRO_HTTP_DRAIN_MS + drain_deadline`; an exchange that outlives the drain is cancelled and classified, which makes every restart, and therefore every credential rotation, a host-wide event for outbound HTTP. A Laravel queue worker parked in a `RESERVE` exits within `block_for` + 1 s plus any job delivered in that window, so supervisord's `stopwaitsecs` must exceed that.]*

## 19. Engine restart & client resilience

A `ferrod` crash or deploy is a *correlated* failure — every worker at once — unlike shared-nothing PDO where connection death is uncorrelated. v0.1 hand-waved this; it is now first-class. The design goal: an engine restart is a **blip for reads and declared-idempotent writes, a clean typed error for everything else, and never a silent unknown**.

### 19.1 Epochs

`boot_epoch` arrives in HELLO_ACK and is cached by the client. On reconnect, a changed epoch means: all engine-side state (tx pins, streams, prepared handles) is gone. Type-registry hash is re-verified as always; mismatch remains a hard error.

*[Amended 2026-10-06 (§24.3): **one deliberate exception.** Ferro Queue's fencing tokens live in the jobs table's rows, not in engine memory, so they survive a `boot_epoch` change: a worker finishes its fenced verbs on the successor daemon. Everything the queue engine holds in memory (wait sets, wake hints) is epoch-scoped as usual.]*

### 19.2 Client reconnect loop

On dead socket (EOF, write failure, missed PONG deadline): reconnect with jittered exponential backoff (base 10 ms, cap 1 s, deadline default 5 s → then `Retryable{ConnectionLost}` surfaces to the caller as pool-unavailable). With socket activation (§18), restarts rarely refuse — connects block in backlog until the new instance accepts.

*[Amended M3-D1c (§22.2 (cp)): the "missed PONG deadline" is now how the client tells a slow statement from a dead engine. A read that waits the transport's read timeout in silence no longer closes the session: the session sends a liveness PING and keeps waiting, and only a further whole read timeout with nothing at all read since that PING went out closes it (so a dead engine is detected in about two read timeouts). A request deadline is opt-in and applies only to a buffered SQL EXEC — `Ferro::connect(statementTimeout: …)` sends `timeout_ms`, which the ENGINE enforces over the wait for a pooled connection and the statement together and answers with the §19.3 fate, and arms a client backstop two seconds later that CANCELs that request; if the engine then sends no terminal within one more read timeout, the session closes. Transaction control, admin requests and streams carry no `timeout_ms` and are bounded by liveness only.]*

### 19.3 Fate rules on connection loss

For each request in flight when the link died:

- **Read** (`readonly: true`, or manifest `readonly`): `Retryable{ConnectionLost, epoch_changed}`. Auto-retried by the client when `retry_reads = true` (default), transparently, on the new epoch.
- **Autocommit write:** `Indeterminate{WriteUnconfirmed, cause: link_lost}`. Auto-retried **only** when the manifest entry declares `idempotent: true`; otherwise surfaced. Raw-SQL writes always surface.
- **In-transaction request:** the whole transaction is dead (the engine rolled it back or died with it). Closure-API users: the closure re-runs on the new epoch under the caller's retry policy — this is why the closure is the primary tx API. Explicit BEGIN/COMMIT users receive `Retryable{ConnectionLost, epoch_changed: true}` and must restart the transaction themselves.
- A `COMMIT` frame sent with no response is the one transactional `Indeterminate`: the commit may or may not have applied. Surfaced as `Indeterminate{cause: link_lost}`; never auto-retried.
- A request whose frame was **never completely written** was never dispatched: `Retryable{ConnectionLost}`, whatever its kind — the COMMIT carve-out above is for a COMMIT frame that was *sent*. This is the engine-side mirror's "not-yet-dispatched" rule applied to the client's half of the link, and it is sound only because the client's session **poisons itself on the first transport failure**: it closes the socket (the engine sees EOF and discards any partial frame) and refuses every later frame before writing a byte, so no later frame can complete an earlier partial one. `Retryable` still licenses only the CALLER: the client never re-issues a write itself. *(M2-C1e-3, §22.2 (bx).)*

Engine-side mirror: when a *backend* link dies, the engine applies the same classification (dispatched-without-response → `Indeterminate`; not-yet-dispatched → `Retryable`) and MUST NOT transparently re-dispatch (§3).

*[Amended 2026-10-06 (D21, §23.7.3, §24.6): **the other services' rows.** An HTTP request in flight when the link dies follows §23.7.3's table: before `HEAD` the client counts it idempotent only if the request itself declared so, a written non-idempotent request with no `HEAD` is `Indeterminate{engine_restart}`, and after `HEAD` the engine's reported idempotency decides. The client never re-issues an HTTP request. Queue verbs follow §24.6: autocommit fenced verbs, a dedup-keyed single-job `ENQUEUE` and `RESERVE` carry D21 licences, and `retry_reads` does **not** apply to `RESERVE`, whose re-send runs under the queue licence, not as a read. A request whose frame was never completely written is `Retryable` for every service, by the rule above.]*

### 19.4 What the engine gives back

The same daemon that concentrates risk also absorbs it: circuit breakers, engine-side backend reconnection, and drain semantics mean a flapping database is met by one polite reconnector instead of 200 stampeding workers, and workers keep getting fast typed `Retryable` errors instead of hanging on TCP timeouts. Documentation should present this chapter as a trade made deliberately, with the mechanics above as the price paid.

## 20. Repository layout, conventions, testing

### 20.1 Layout

```
/engine                     Cargo workspace
  crates/ferro-proto        wire types, codec, generated consts, vector tests
  crates/ferro-classify     assist lexer (§7.1)
  crates/ferro-pool         generic pool + pin state machine
  crates/ferro-backend-pg   | -mysql | -sqlite | -mssql
  crates/ferrod             daemon binary (session layer, services, admin)
  crates/ferro-cli          `ferro` CLI (schema sync / check / gen)
/php
  client/                   ferro/client            (PSR-4 `Ferro\`)
  doctrine-dbal/            ferro/doctrine-dbal-driver
  laravel/                  ferro/laravel
/proto                      methods.toml, errors.toml, types.toml, vectors/
/bench                      harness + committed results with env manifests
/testkit                    docker-compose (pg, mysql, mssql), fixtures,
                            upstream-suite runners (DBAL, illuminate/database)
```

*[Amended 2026-10-06 (§23.13, §24.16 C6): v1 adds `engine/crates/ferro-http` (Ferro HTTP, behind the default-on cargo feature `http`; D20), `engine/crates/ferro-queue` (store model and per-dialect statement builders, no I/O), `ferrod`'s `services/queue.rs` and `queue/waker.rs`, and the PHP packages `php/guzzle` (`ferro/guzzle`) and `php/psr18` (`ferro/psr18`). The Laravel wiring for both engines lives in `php/laravel`. A spike crate such as `ferro-http-spike` ships no library code, as `ferro-sqlite-spike` does.]*

### 20.2 Conventions

Rust: latest stable pinned in `rust-toolchain.toml`, edition 2024, tokio multi-thread, `thiserror` in libs / `anyhow` at binary edges, `tracing` throughout, clippy warnings denied in CI, `cargo-fuzz` on every codec/lexer. PHP: ≥ 8.2, PSR-12, PHPStan level 9 on `client/`, PHPUnit 10. The `/proto` registry is the only place protocol numbers exist; hand-written constants in either language are a review reject.

### 20.3 Testing strategy

- **Conformance:** golden frame vectors in `/proto/vectors/`; the PHP codec MUST byte-match them (both codec paths: pure and `ext-msgpack`).
- **Engine:** unit tests per crate; integration tests against dockerized backends via `/testkit`; pin-engine tests assert pin cause labels for each trigger class, incl. the M1 MySQL tracker-in-procedure verification (§7.1).
- **Chaos:** a harness that SIGKILLs `ferrod` mid-workload and asserts the §19.3 fate matrix — every in-flight request must land in exactly the specified branch. This is the acceptance test for G7.
- **Upstream suites:** `/testkit` runners execute the DBAL and `illuminate/database` suites against Ferro connections; green is the M1/M2 acceptance bar, run in CI nightly. *[Amended M2 (§22.3): **not nightly.** The DBAL, `illuminate/database` and doctrine/orm suites (`testkit/orm-suite.sh`, added in M2) run from ON-DEMAND workflows (`dbal-suite`, `laravel-suite`, `orm-suite`), because each takes tens of minutes per column and needs pinned upstream trees. The per-push `ci` workflow carries the engine, client, tier and §15 demo-app gates. The Laravel suite's SQLite columns are measured in the dev container (the workflow has no SQLite option).]*
- **Bench:** harness runs in CI as informational; the D12 gate at M0 exit is a human sign-off on recorded numbers.

*[Amended 2026-10-06 (§23.12, §23.14, §24.12, §24.13): **the chaos bar extends to both engines.** Ferro HTTP's chaos suite runs against a fault-injecting upstream that records every request it received, so at-most-once is a read-back assertion (§23.14); Ferro Queue's runs against real Dockerized backends and ends every scenario with a read-back over the rows (§24.13). Their drop-in bars: for HTTP, Lane B (`php-http/client-integration-tests`' `HttpClientTest`) and Lane C (a Ferro-authored, stock-controlled conformance file), with Symfony's `HttpClientTestCase` (Lane S) the first post-v1 lane; for Queue, three Laravel columns per family (stock `database` over `pdo_*`, stock `database` over `ferro-*`, the `ferro` driver) plus the §15 demo's queue paths. Every column carries contact assertions in both directions (D18).]*

## 21. Decision log & open items

| # | Decision | Rationale / revisit trigger |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | MSSQL pools default to **session mode** until tiberius reset-on-RPC semantics are verified (M4 spike). | Correctness over pooling wins; flip default when verified. |
| D2 | DBAL **`^4.0` first** (M1 acceptance); `^3.8` bridge in M2. | Cuts a third of the SPI shims out of the critical path; Symfony 6 adoption pressure decides how hard M2 pushes. |
| D3 | Query extraction v1 = `.sql` files + attributes; PHPStan inline-literal extraction = phase 2. | Deterministic sources first; static analysis is a quality-of-life add. |
| D4 | Windows dev = TCP loopback only; memfd disabled; named pipes out of scope v1. | Not a production target. |
| D5 | `quote()` implemented client-side with per-platform tables; no engine round trip. | A network hop inside a discouraged compat API is absurd; tables are small and testable. **HELD, after being briefly broken and then satisfied (M2-C2f → C2g):** C2f's Eloquent-tier `quote()` was client-side but verified `standard_conforming_strings` with one cached `SHOW`, a round trip D5 forbids. C2g removed it — `HELLO_ACK` now advertises `literals_are_standard` per pool, which the engine learns from a `GUC_REPORT` `ParameterStatus` at zero cost, so quoting sends nothing (asserted on the session's own sent-frame record). The verification itself is NOT optional and must not be removed to "simplify": an unverified premise under an escaping function was the one alternative considered and rejected. §22.2 (as), (at). |
| D6 | No at-rest encryption of memfd buffers in v1. | Sealed anonymous memory + `SO_PEERCRED`-gated fd passing is the boundary; revisit if multi-tenant hosts become a target. |
| D7 | "Ferro" remains a placeholder. **Maintainer task before M1:** crates.io / Packagist / trademark check. | Human-only item. |
| D8 | No credential passthrough from engine to PHP tooling (`schema:dump`, `artisan db`, dump-based backup packages). Documented incompat + ops-provisioned dump credentials. | Credential isolation is a headline guarantee; punching a hole for convenience inverts it. Revisit as a scoped admin "credential lease" only with real demand. |
| D9 | The pool is hand-rolled, not `deadpool`/`bb8`. | The pin state machine, conditional hygiene, and checkout-pipelining *are* the product; generic pools fight all three. |
| D10 | `ferro` CLI is a Rust binary in the workspace. | Shares proto + backend crates; shadow-DB PREPARE needs real drivers. |
| D11 | `COMPRESSED` flag reserved; no implementation before post-M3 bench evidence. | UDS loopback rarely wants zstd; keep the bit, skip the work. |
| D12 | Codec strategy is empirical: pure PHP + `ext-msgpack` autodetect at M0; `ext-php-rs` accelerator pulled into M1 iff the M0 p99 gate fails. | §16.1. |
| D13 | **SQLite write serialization is declaration-driven, never inferred.** Every transaction takes the writer lock at `BEGIN` (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`) unless the request DECLARED `readonly`, which takes a deferred reader lock instead; autocommit statements follow the same rule once `readonly` reaches the pool. WAL + `busy_timeout` are set regardless. | §7.6's original "one writer serialized engine-side" could not be implemented: identifying writers IS the read/write inference charter rule 6 and §3 forbid. Option A (`busy_timeout` alone) rejected as a *design* — it leaves the deferred-upgrade case, where a transaction that began as a reader and then writes gets `SQLITE_BUSY_SNAPSHOT`, which `busy_timeout` does **not** retry and which needs the rollback-and-replay rule 3 forbids the engine performing. Taking the lock at BEGIN eliminates that class BY CONSTRUCTION; honouring the client's existing `readonly` recovers read concurrency with no inference anywhere. **Cost stated, not discovered later:** a tier that declares every statement a write gets no read concurrency at all — the Doctrine tier does exactly that (§22.2 (ac)), so C3 buys it nothing until it declares honestly, and improves on its own as tiers learn to. **Revisit trigger:** none for the rule. The live question is the SEAM — `readonly` reaches `ferrod`'s SQL service but NOT `ferro-pool` (it appears there only in two comments), so autocommit read routing needs a new `Pool::checkout` seam, and that same seam is what single-flight read coalescing and read-your-writes replica routing would each need. `docs/dev-loop/C3-SQLITE-SCOPE.md` §1; §22.2 (az). |
| D14 | **The engine confines every file it opens on a client's behalf to one directory per pool.** `ATTACH DATABASE '<path>'` and `VACUUM INTO '<path>'` are permitted, but only within the pool's `allow_dir`, which DEFAULTS to the database file's own directory and is widened by the operator via `FERRO_POOL_<NAME>_ALLOW_DIR`. Enforced by SQLite's own authorizer callback, never by a statement-text denylist. | §12/D8 keeps the database path in the engine so PHP never learns it, and these two statements hand that choice back — the client names a path and the ENGINE writes a complete copy of the database there. That is a CONFUSED DEPUTY rather than a leak of data the client could not already read: the write happens as **ferrod's** user, which under §18's systemd deployment is not PHP-FPM's, so a client directs an authority it does not itself hold. **Refusing `VACUUM INTO` by name was rejected as security theatre, and only a measurement showed why:** `ATTACH` plus an ordinary `CREATE TABLE side.copy AS SELECT …` inside one transaction produces the identical copy at the identical path, and `ATTACH` is permitted by design ((be)'s hygiene list exists to `DETACH` it). The negative control makes it precise — outside a transaction the attachment does not survive to the next statement, because `ATTACH` taints unconditionally ((bm)) — so the TRANSACTION carries the capability, not the verb, and any per-verb refusal would have closed nothing. **The authorizer is the mechanism because it cannot rot** (the (bm) reasoning again): both verbs arrive as the SAME `SQLITE_ATTACH` event carrying the RESOLVED filename before SQLite opens anything, so one guard covers both and any future verb that attaches a file, with no SQL parsing. Charter rule 6 is untouched — nothing is rewritten and nothing is inferred from statement text; SQLite states which file it is about to open and the engine answers. **The default is what makes this safe without configuration:** a snapshot beside the database works out of the box, so no existing deployment changes, and reaching a backup volume is an operator decision. *(Corrected at M2-C3-7b, measured: this sentence first read "`VACUUM INTO 'snap.db'` beside the database works out of the box", which is true only when `ferrod`'s working directory IS the database's — SQLite resolves a RELATIVE name against the process's cwd, and the guard rightly resolves it the same way, so under any other cwd it is refused. The absolute spelling works; the admin `BACKUP` verb never depends on cwd. And the guard now refuses three things it could not check, wherever they point (§22.2 (cf)): a target that is itself a SYMLINK — canonicalising only the parent let a dangling link redirect a snapshot outside the directory, and a link to an existing outside database be READ through `ATTACH`; an `ATTACH` whose target is not a literal — SQLite passes the authorizer NO filename for a bound parameter or an expression, and the guard used to allow what it could not see; and a `file:` URI — the bundled SQLite honours URIs on every connection and decodes their percent-escapes after the guard has looked.)* **Cost, stated:** an application that `ATTACH`es a database outside that directory must have the operator widen `allow_dir`; plain `VACUUM` (which stock Laravel's `dropAllTables()` ends in) is untouched, since it opens no file. **Alternatives rejected:** leaving it open (accepts the confused deputy under the deployment §18 describes) and refusing path-naming on the SQL service entirely (breaks `ATTACH` for applications that legitimately use it, which is a drop-in violation, and buys nothing this does not). §22.2 (bo), (bp); `docs/dev-loop/C3-SQLITE-SCOPE.md` §21–22. |
| D15 | **The admin service is UDS-only in M2, authorized by peer uid, with OPERATE verbs gated separately from READ verbs.** Admin verbs (`ADMIN = 5`) are served only on the engine's Unix socket, where `SO_PEERCRED` supplies a kernel-attested uid — the uid of the process that CREATED the connection, which an fd inherited across `fork` + `setuid` keeps. The accept-time gate (`FERRO_ALLOW_UIDS`) is unchanged and still decides who may connect at all. READ verbs (pool and engine state) are open to any admitted peer, and **may return only what §12 and product-vision §5's redaction contract permit** — no DSN, credential, raw SQL, parameter value or row data; a verb that would return any of those is OPERATE or is not built. OPERATE verbs — anything that writes, changes engine state or returns protected material, starting with §7.6's online backup — additionally require the peer uid in `FERRO_ADMIN_UIDS`, and **an empty `FERRO_ADMIN_UIDS` means OPERATE is disabled**: there is no implicit member. A connection with no kernel-attested uid — any TCP transport, should one ever serve `/proto` — is refused every admin verb. Cross-host admin is not built in M2. D15 does not decide §18's `/healthz` transport. | **Owner decision, 2026-10-02**, with the default amended the same day under the owner's grant of freedom on M2's choices. Chosen over a dedicated admin socket (isolation by path: a second socket and unit for nothing the uid split does not give) and a bearer token now (secret management with no cross-host consumer). **Why OPERATE defaults to DISABLED rather than "self-only"** (the first draft's reading, refuted by adversarial review): `uid_allowed` admits ONLY the daemon's own uid when `FERRO_ALLOW_UIDS` is empty and does NOT include it once the list is set, so "empty = self" gave every same-uid PHP worker every OPERATE verb in the default deployment, and made OPERATE unreachable in the production one. **A uid split is only as real as the deployment's uids:** if PHP workers run as the same uid as the admin, no uid-based model can tell them apart, and D15 does not pretend to. An OPERATE uid must also pass `FERRO_ALLOW_UIDS`, so it holds the data plane too — accepted, because the first OPERATE verb produces a full copy of the database anyway. **What the gate is NOT:** snapshot confinement. Under D14 any admitted peer can already `VACUUM INTO` a path inside the pool's `allow_dir` over the data plane; the OPERATE gate governs the admin verb's own powers (its destination policy and any destructive replace). Revisit trigger: any consumer that needs admin verbs across hosts (product-vision's fleet console), at which point mTLS/bearer per product-vision §5. |
| D16 | **v1 is the database engine PLUS the HTTP transport engine and the job transport engine** (owner decision, 2026-10-06). v1 ships §17's M3–M5 for the database engine, then **Ferro HTTP** (product-vision §4.2: named upstreams, daemon-side TLS and connection pools, the fate taxonomy, the Guzzle `HandlerStack`/PSR-18 seam, SSE/chunked streaming), then **Ferro Queue** (§4.3: fronts PG `SKIP LOCKED` first, never owns a durable log, transactional enqueue on the business write's `tx_id`, the Laravel Queue and Symfony Messenger seams). Each engine gets its own SPEC section and its own service id before any code, and passes the product-vision §3 admission test in writing. Order and slicing are §17's (amended). | Supersedes product-vision P1's "v1 stays undiluted" and §4.2's start trigger ("database engine at v1 … real production adopters"), recorded as P13. **Cost stated:** v1 moves out by the size of two engines, and both engines ship without the production-demand signal the doctrine waited for. The chassis rule holds: nothing SQL-specific enters the chassis, and nothing HTTP- or queue-specific enters it either. *[Amended 2026-10-06 (§24.17 Q4, decided under the owner's full-freedom grant): the Symfony Messenger seam named above is post-v1. Ferro Queue's v1 drop-in tier is Laravel's queue.]* |
| D17 | **The §16 reference environment is a GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-latest` runner, with the environment manifest recorded per run** (owner decision, 2026-10-06). Every §16 number is taken on that runner class, at least twice, with the manifest (CPU model, core count, kernel, PHP version and JIT status, backend versions) committed beside the result in `bench/results/`. | Available and reproducible now; closes the maintainer open item "reference-hardware sign-off for §16". **Cost stated:** shared runners are noisier than bare metal, so a p99 measured there is an upper bound on the target's p99, and the record says so. A bare-metal re-run can be added later without changing the bar. |
| D18 | **"Green" for an upstream suite means parity with the stock driver** (owner decision, 2026-10-06). A suite column is green when every test the stock-driver control passes also passes through Ferro, the skip sets match (or every difference is shown to be driver-name gated), and every Ferro-named non-pass is triaged as a driver-name artifact or a documented incompatibility in `docs/known-incompatibilities.md`. A Ferro-only failure with neither explanation is a defect, and the column is not green. | Upstream tests branch on the driver NAME (§22.2 (am), (ar)), so literal zero-failure under Ferro's own names is unreachable without editing upstream. This is the measurable form of drop-in. It requires a control column for every family a suite claims, so §22.3's missing DBAL server-family controls become required work. |
| D19 | **Ferro HTTP's contract** (decided under the owner's full-freedom grant as applied 2026-10-06 (ledger, "Owner directives and grants"); normative text §23). The HTTP transport engine (service `HTTP = 6`) sends a request only to an operator-declared upstream, at its declared origin and checked address class, with an origin-form target validated byte by byte and never rewritten (§23.4). It attaches daemon-held credentials from files, refusing a PHP value for an attached name unless the operator opts into override, and never sends a request twice (charter rule 3). A completed exchange is `Outcome::Ok` whatever its status; status classification is an advisory client helper. Fate follows §23.7: `sent` is measured at the HTTP/1.1 connection's plaintext write, and `Indeterminate` is exactly *non-idempotent ∧ sent ∧ no response head*. In v1 a non-idempotent request never rides HTTP/2. Effective idempotency is a declaration (the caller's, or the operator's per upstream), never the method alone. | The §3 admission test is passed in writing in §23.1, and the fate table and the SSRF rule were attacked by an adversarial review before adoption (§22.2 (cn)). The choices fixed with it (§23.18): environment-variable configuration; HTTP/1.1 by default; `IDEMPOTENT_METHODS` empty by default; no configuration reload in v1, so rotation is a restart behind a 30 s HTTP drain; no large-request-body path; no Symfony `HttpClientInterface` in v1; the acceptance bar is §23.12's Lane B plus Lane C. **Cost stated:** with the defaults an undeclared `GET` that dies after sending is `Indeterminate` to Ferro-aware deciders (§22.2 (ac)'s trade); writes get no HTTP/2 multiplexing; every restart is a host-wide event for exchanges that outlive the drain; the host presents one client identity per upstream. **Revisit triggers:** spike F1b proves the HTTP/2 not-processed licences with their negative control (unlocking writes over HTTP/2 post-v1); an upstream class whose fate a response head does not settle. |
| D20 | **The HTTP dependency set** (decided under the owner's full-freedom grant as applied 2026-10-06 (ledger, "Owner directives and grants"); §23.13). `hyper` 1.x (connection-level client only), `rustls` with `ring`, OS roots via `rustls-native-certs`, and `unicode-normalization`, isolated in the crate `ferro-http` behind the default-on cargo feature `http` and run in-process inside `ferrod`, gated by the full `cargo deny check` (advisories included) on both feature configurations. TLS and HTTP parsing are never hand-rolled; OTLP and metrics stay hand-rolled. | TLS cannot be hand-rolled, and here the input is adversarial (response heads, chunked framing, HPACK from the internet), which is the reason to use the most deployed and fuzzed parsers rather than to avoid them. That is the opposite of the one-shape, trusted-peer case that kept OTLP and metrics hand-rolled (§22.2 (ce), whose "no HTTP stack in the daemon" still stands for those). `ring` over `aws-lc-rs`, because `aws-lc-sys` needs CMake and carries the `OpenSSL` licence `deny.toml` does not allow; OS roots over `webpki-roots`, a stale snapshot under a licence not allowed. A separate process was declined as a chassis change with no measured need. **Cost stated:** the credential-holding daemon now links a TLS and HTTP stack; `--no-default-features` builds a database-only daemon for an operator who wants none of it. **Revisit triggers:** an unpatchable advisory in this tree; a measured reason to move the engine into its own process. |
| D21 | **Retry licences are defined per service, and none is an inference** (decided under the owner's full-freedom grant as applied 2026-10-06 (ledger, "Owner directives and grants"); one entry for §23 and §24). Manifest `idempotent: true` (§11) is the sole licence to auto-retry an `Indeterminate` **SQL statement**. Every other service defines its licences in its own section — §23.7.2 for Ferro HTTP, §24.6 for Ferro Queue — and each licence is either a **declaration** (by the caller or by the operator) or a property the **protocol** defines for an operation whose statements the engine itself composes. None is ever inferred from an HTTP method or from user statement text. A licence permits only the caller's policy layer to retry; the engine never re-sends anything (charter rule 3). | §9.2, §11 and §19.3 called the manifest flag "the sole license", which a second service could not satisfy without an inference or a manifest it does not have. HTTP licenses through classification, and nothing licenses re-sending an `Indeterminate` HTTP request: a request the caller or the operator declared idempotent is never `Indeterminate` — a link-level failure after sending is `Retryable`, and a timeout or cancel follows §9.2's read rule — and the method alone licenses nothing (§22.2 (ac)'s rule, applied to HTTP). Queue's licences are protocol properties of engine-composed verbs: an autocommit fenced verb re-sent with the same token (the fence admits at most one application, and the re-send's answer resolves the first send's fate), a dedup-keyed single-job `ENQUEUE`, and `RESERVE` (a re-send is a new reservation whose worst case is that a lost lease expires). Both engine drafts had numbered this decision D21; it is one entry. It narrows nothing for SQL. **Revisit trigger:** a licence proposed for any service that would rest on interpreting a request's method, target, body or statement text, rather than on a field the caller or the operator declared (the presence of an operator-declared `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_HEADER` in a request is such a declared field, §23.7.2 (c)). |
| D22 | **Ferro Queue's closed statement set is a scoped exception to §3 and charter rule 6 — PENDING OWNER RATIFICATION** (decided under the owner's full-freedom grant as applied 2026-10-06 (ledger, "Owner directives and grants"); because it amends binding scope text, no G-slice code (G1 onward) starts before the owner ratifies it; §24.16 C8). The job transport engine (§24) composes a closed, per-dialect set of statements against an operator-declared table whose layout the application owns, and may write that table's rows on its own authority only as §24.2 I4 enumerates — in v1, restoring a reservation no client received ("unreserve") and purging expired keys from the operator-created dedup table. No user statement is read, rewritten or inferred from. Charter rule 6 reads with the sentence "Engine-authored statements of the §24 queue verbs are not SQL rewriting, and the `ferro` queue driver is not a drop-in database tier under the rule's 'change execution, never SQL generation': it is a new driver name whose transport replaces `DatabaseQueue`'s builder-generated SQL by design, while the drop-in database tiers keep stock SQL generation." | v1 as redefined by D16 includes a queue engine, and it cannot exist without this: none of the earlier precedents (`compose_begin_sql`, savepoint names, hygiene, the version probe, `VACUUM INTO`) writes rows in an application table. It is a transport over application-owned storage, not storage owned by the engine, so product-vision rule E ("never owns storage") holds. **Cost stated:** a defect in an engine statement builder can now damage application rows. The mitigations are shape verification at first use, the fence on every engine write, and mutation-proven builders in the no-I/O crate `ferro-queue`. **Revisit trigger:** any new engine-initiated write — liveness release first (§24.15) — needs its own decision, because this one does not cover it. *(`CLAUDE.md`'s copy of charter rule 6 gains the sentence above when that file is next updated; this decision binds meanwhile.)* |

**Open items (maintainer):** license selection; naming (D7); security review scheduling before any public beta. *(Reference-hardware sign-off for §16 was settled by D17.)*

## 22. Changelog v0.1 → v0.2

- Pin detection inverted: backend protocol signals (PG ReadyForQuery, MySQL session trackers) are primary; lexer demoted to assist role for protocol-invisible statements (§7.1).
- Hygiene moved to checkout (pipelined, RTT hidden) and made conditional; targeted PG profile fixed to release advisory locks and temp state; MySQL reset skipped on tracker-clean connections, reconciling reset semantics with the 95 % cache target (§7.2).
- Transaction-mode contract stated: function-side session mutation unsupported on PG, `pin_functions` + session mode as escape hatches (§7.4).
- Error taxonomy gained the `Indeterminate` branch; write-fate rules defined for cancel/timeout/link-loss (§9.2).
- New §19: boot epochs, client reconnect loop, fate matrix, closure-as-recovery-surface, no transparent engine retries; chaos test mandated (§20.3).
- Manifest schema gained `readonly` + `idempotent`; idempotence is the sole auto-retry license for indeterminate writes (§11).
- §5.1 memfd corrected: one client-side copy via `ext-sockets`/`php://fd`, gated on `MEMFD_RX`; zero-copy is engine-side until the native accelerator exists.
- §5.2 added: normative request_id, CANCEL/END race, per-request + per-session credit, PING/GOODBYE.
- §16.1 added: component-level latency budget, p99 risk analysis, D12 decision gate.
- §18 rewritten: templated `ferrod@` units + socket activation holding the listener fd; old/new coexistence during rollout.
- Doctrine/Eloquent incompat lists extended with credential-dependent tooling (D8); DBAL 4-first (D2).
- Min PHP raised to 8.2 (readonly classes). All v0.1 §19 open questions resolved or defaulted in §21.

### 22.1 M0 implementation deviations (thin-slice scope)

These record where the M0 build (milestone §17.1) intentionally implements a *subset* of the target design above; each is a scope/milestone note, not a change to the target spec. See `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-23-ferro-m0-execution-design.md` §4 for the decision context.

- **§20.1 workspace** — single repo-root Cargo workspace (`engine/crates/*` + `bench`) instead of an `/engine`-rooted one, so the charter commands run verbatim (B-1).
- **§7.1 / §17.1(4) pin stub (M-2, CLOSED in M1-S1)** — the M0 pin stub was driven from the TX-service lifecycle, not from the PG ReadyForQuery I/T/E byte (stock `tokio-postgres` exposes none). RFQ-byte access was raised as a §21 open item for the M1 pin engine (M-2). **Resolved in M1-S1** (decision M1-D2, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-ferro-m1-execution-design.md`): a vendored `tokio-postgres` fork (`vendor/tokio-postgres`, root `Cargo.toml` `[patch.crates-io]`) exposes the real ReadyForQuery status byte via `Client::transaction_status()`, wired through `PoolBackend::tx_status` into `Checkout::apply_tx_status` (`ferro-pool`) as the pool's pin AUTHORITY — the engine's own manual pin bookkeeping (`begin_tx_with`/`commit_tx`/`rollback_tx`'s hand-set fields, the S6 actor's teardown `set_tainted(true)`) is retained as defense-in-depth, not the authority. The upstream PR is drafted, not yet filed (`/UPSTREAM_PR.md`, pending maintainer sign-off/human authorization). **The fork has since grown and the drop condition grew with it (M1-S8a review round):** it now carries THREE Ferro additions — `transaction_status()` (this one), `parameter()`, and `clear_typeinfo_statement_cache()` (§22.2 (m)) — so the fork and this note are dropped only once **all three** are upstream, which is what `/UPSTREAM_PR.md`'s checklist now requires.
- **Charter rule 6 / §6 placeholders** — an engine-side mechanical `?`→`$n` scanner is accepted as parameter-syntax normalization (not SQL rewriting); a bare jsonb `?` must be written `??` or the client emits native `$n` (M-1).
- **§20.2 tooling** — PHPUnit 11 (not 10); the M0 client advertises `features=0` (B-2).
- **§7.2 hygiene** — M0 does minimal hygiene (release-time `ROLLBACK` guard only, applied at the *next* checkout since `Drop` is sync); full conditional/pipelined hygiene is M1. The D12 `SELECT 1` path is unaffected (R8). (M1-S3 note: post-conditional-hygiene, a warm PG recycle now runs one awaited Targeted reset at checkout — see §22.2; the D12 hot-path cost is revisited at the deferred bare-metal D12 re-run.)
- **§5.2 / §6 EXEC framing (D-S5-1)** — M0's EXEC **buffers** each result into the single `Outcome::Ok` terminal frame; the windowed streaming DATA-channel producer (per-request credit wakeup, per-session cap accounting/release, cross-channel terminal ordering, HEAD/DATA framing, `fetch:stream`) is **deferred to post-M0**. Building it before the D12 bench (§16.1) demands it is speculative optimization (charter rule 5). A result whose encoded terminal body exceeds `MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD` → `NonRetryable{Unsupported}`. The Indeterminate write-fate guarantee (§19.3) is upheld in M0: a non-readonly conn-loss surfaces `WriteUnconfirmed{Indeterminate}`, never an auto-retry.
- **§5 HELLO_ACK pool discovery (S7 Task 2, CLOSED)** — `HelloAck.pools` now advertises the configured pool NAMES (`config.pools[].name`), so a client discovers referenceable pools from the handshake (PROTOCOL.md §4). The S5 stub hardcoded `pools: vec![]`; `hello_ack_frame` gained a `pool_names` param populated at the single `session/mod.rs` call site. Only names cross the wire — never DSNs (§12 server secret). Proven end-to-end by the PHP live handshake test.
- **§7 TX-service known limitation (S6 orphan-BEGIN race)** — if a session's reader loop ends (EOF/GOODBYE/fatal) while a `BEGIN` handler is between `pool.checkout()` and `tx_registry.register()`, the actor can be registered *after* `abort_session` snapshotted the registry, orphaning it under a dead `SessionId`. It is **self-healing and client-invisible**: the actor reaps itself via its `idle_in_tx` deadline (≤ default 10 s → rollback + release the pooled conn + release the semaphore permit + tombstone), the tombstone is `TOMBSTONE_CAP`-bounded and owner-scoped to an unreachable, never-reused id, and there is no double/zero-`END`, no cross-session reachability, and no permit leak beyond `idle_in_tx`. The airtight fix (have `handle_begin` re-check the request `CancellationToken` immediately before `register`, rolling back + dropping the fresh conn if the session is draining) is **deferred to S7-era hardening** — it changes no wire contract and nothing downstream depends on it.
- **§16.1 / §17.1(8) D12 measurement recorded (S8, M0 exit gate MET — provisional)** — the D12 boundary-latency measurement is committed under `bench/results/` with a complete environment manifest, and `ci/check-d12-recorded.sh` exits 0. The recorded run (`bench/README.md`) is tagged **`provisional: true, reference: false`**: it was measured on **WSL2** with a **host-process release `ferrod` + host PHP against the Dockerized testkit Postgres** (the S8 plan's directed topology). On this environment the §16.1 boundary targets (p50 < 60 µs, p99 < 200 µs) are **not met** — the absolute numbers are dominated by the WSL2 + Docker-proxy Postgres round trip, which costs ~678 µs p50 even for the raw PDO baseline; Ferro's *added* overhead vs PDO in the identical environment is ~163–170 µs p50 / ~109–124 µs p99. Per §16.1/§20.3 D12 is a **human sign-off on recorded numbers, not a CI threshold**: the two outstanding sign-off items are (1) a **bare-metal / host-network reference re-run** (the fully-containerized topology of the execution-design is part of that scope), and (2) the **`ext-php-rs` accelerator** decision (the pure-PHP `PurePacker` is what M0 measures; `ext-msgpack` is absent here and its swap is unbuilt). Neither is an M0 blocker — the exit condition is the *recording*, which is met.

### 22.2 M1 implementation deviations

These record where an M1 slice's build intentionally differs from, or narrows/widens, the target design in the body of this spec; each is a scope note against the M1 execution design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-ferro-m1-execution-design.md`), not a silent change to the target spec text.

**How to write and read one (learned from M1-S8a, whose review found two entries flatly contradicting each other).** Twelve S8a tasks wrote entries here in parallel, each blind to the others: (u) ended by stating no `PoolRegistry` was threaded anywhere while (v), written days later by the task that threaded it, said the opposite — and a later slice reading (u) would have re-derived a wire field the whole point of (v) was to give one source. Three rules follow. **(1) A later entry that falsifies an earlier one must go back and CORRECT the earlier one**, in place and labelled, not merely state the truth in its own entry — the reader who needs it will be reading the earlier one. **(2) Corrections QUOTE what they retract** rather than silently rewriting it, so an artifact citing the old text can be recognised as stale rather than as a second opinion. **(3) A claim that something is measured, guarded or proven names the artifact**, and a claim of the form "this is not observable / cannot be guarded / was not worth writing" is the one most worth attacking before it is written. S8a produced three and all three fell: "no pool-level guard is possible for the isolation leak" (the in-transaction guard was built — (s)), "detached vs dropped probe futures has no black-box observable" (the idle stack is one — (v)), and "the version-skew delivery test is carried, deliberately not written" (it is 25 lines — (u)). Whenever this section and the spec BODY disagree, the body is what the next slice reads as the contract, so fix both.

- **§7.2 hygiene profile predicate is `tainted`, not "was pinned" (M1-S3)** — §7.2's literal rule is "a connection that was *pinned* during its last lease → full reset." The shipped predicate (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-28-ferro-m1-s3-conditional-hygiene.md`, S3) is instead the connection's existing `tainted` bit: a cleanly-committed transaction (`BEGIN; SELECT; COMMIT`, no session-mutating statement, no error) does **not** set `tainted` (S1's `commit_tx` clears `tx_open` and leaves `tainted` alone), so it is treated as a non-tainted recycled conn and gets the **targeted** profile, not a forced `DISCARD ALL`. This is a deliberate refinement, not a laxer reading: prepares survive checkout for clean transactions, while the targeted profile still closes every leak class the full reset would have caught. A transaction that *did* mutate session state is `tainted` by the S2 assist lexer (or by an error / aborted transaction via the S1 Err-arm / RFQ `E`) and still gets the full reset.
- **§7.2 targeted profile string deviates from this spec's authored 5-statement list (M1-S3)** — §7.2 authors the targeted profile as `RESET ALL; UNLISTEN *; SELECT pg_advisory_unlock_all(); DISCARD TEMP; DISCARD SEQUENCES;`. The shipped profile (S3) additionally runs `CLOSE ALL` and `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION DEFAULT` ahead of that list. `CLOSE ALL` closes a gap found during S3 verification: a `WITH HOLD` cursor (`DECLARE`, session-scoped, survives `COMMIT`) is not a safe-listed leading keyword, so under `pin_on_unknown=false` a `DECLARE ... WITH HOLD` conn classifies non-tainted — without `CLOSE ALL` the cursor (and the rows it can `FETCH`) would leak to the next tenant. `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION DEFAULT` matches `DISCARD ALL`'s separate handling of `role`/`session_authorization` (which `RESET ALL` alone does not cover); it is a reset-to-self and never errors. With both additions the shipped targeted profile is **functionally equivalent to** `DISCARD ALL` minus its two prepare-affecting statements (`DEALLOCATE ALL`/`DISCARD PLANS`, which stay omitted so namespaced prepares survive a targeted reset), with the advisory-lock release made explicit via `SELECT pg_advisory_unlock_all()`. Of that pair only `DEALLOCATE ALL` destroys the statements; `DISCARD PLANS` drops their cached PLANS and deallocates nothing (measured on PG 17: `PREPARE zzp` → `pg_prepared_statements` has 1 row, `DISCARD PLANS` → still 1, `DEALLOCATE ALL` → 0). Both are still rightly omitted — discarding the plans of the prepares the profile exists to preserve would throw away the work that makes preserving them worthwhile — but several M1-S3-era comments called them jointly "the two prepare-destroying statements", which is false of `DISCARD PLANS`; corrected in the M1-S8a review round. Recommend updating §7.2's authored list itself to match in a future spec pass.
- **§7.2 "pipelined ahead of the first user statement" is deferred post-S3 (M1-S3)** — S3 ships the conditional Full-vs-Targeted profile selection and the live leak-prevention + prepares-survive proof, but not checkout-pipelined hygiene: hygiene continues to run **awaited at checkout**, exactly as before S3, only with the corrected per-state profile. The optimization is deferred to a later perf slice, gated on both a recorded bench number and the §7.3 statement cache (which is unbuilt — every query currently re-`prepare`s, so pipelining has no cached prepares to protect yet) and on not touching the fork's single-slot RFQ atomic invariant to sequence it. This is charter rule 5 (no speculative optimization), not a capability gap; see the amended S3 gate in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-ferro-m1-execution-design.md`.
- **§4/§7 session-death teardown of an in-flight tx-scoped statement may resolve via the per-request `cancel` arm, not only via `abort` (M1-S4)** — the M1-S4 plan's T3 constraint (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-28-ferro-m1-s4-fate-matrix.md` T3) states that `session/mod.rs`'s `cancel_all()` at session death must route an in-flight tx-scoped `Exec` through the actor's `abort` teardown (deregister + drop-reply) only, NOT through the new per-request rollback+tombstone+reply path. As shipped, this is not quite what happens: the actor's new per-request `cancel: CancellationToken` (`tx::TxCommand::Exec`) is, of structural necessity, the SAME token `session::registry::Registry::cancel_all()` fires (it is the one token a client's own `CANCEL{request_id}` must also reach — giving the tx actor a second, separate token here would sever that routing). `session/mod.rs::run_with_handler` calls `registry.cancel_all()` *before* `tx_registry.abort_session(session_id)` (§4 ordering, unchanged by this slice), so on session death the actor's per-request `cancel` arm can win the race against `abort`, tearing the transaction down via `TxEnd::Deadline` (rollback + tombstone + a declared `TxDeadline{Retryable}` reply) instead of `TxEnd::Abort` (rollback + deregister + a silently-dropped reply). The literal fix is a session-layer reorder or a second, distinct per-request token threaded to the actor solely for this path — both out of S4's scope (a control-flow change to the session supervisor, not a fate-matrix change) — so this is recorded as a deviation, not silently patched around. It is **benign**: traced exhaustively (see the M1-S4 Task 3 report) — exactly one `teardown()` runs either way (conn always rolled back + released, permit never leaked), the forwarding handler still declares exactly one terminal (§5.2 holds), the losing client (already disconnecting) sees `TxDeadline{Retryable}` rather than `Protocol` — safe-or-better, since the tx is already rolled back either way (no double-apply risk), and the resulting tombstone is bounded by `TOMBSTONE_CAP` and scoped to a dead, never-reused `SessionId` (§7's `abort_session` final `retain` purges it regardless of Abort vs. Deadline). The non-cancel-statement-error (e.g. `23505`) no-auto-rollback behavior (§9.2, the S6 contract) is unaffected — this deviation is scoped to the interleaving of two teardown *signals*, not to the statement-error fate rules.
- **§5.2 `default_credit_bytes` coupled to `MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD` (M1-S5 Task 1b, user-confirmed Option B)** — the streaming DATA-channel producer's large-row hazard (a single indivisible row up to `MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD`, but the client will not replenish a request's credit window before it has seen that frame) is closed by COUPLING the default per-request byte window to the frame ceiling: `default_credit_bytes` changed from 4 MiB to 16 MiB (`== MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD`, `proto/methods.toml`), and `ferrod::config::Config::validate` now fail-fasts at startup if either `credit_bytes` or `session_cap_bytes` is configured below `MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD`. This **supersedes** the earlier `config.rs` "do NOT couple `credit_bytes`/`session_cap_bytes` to `MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD`" intent noted during S5 setup — that note predates the large-row hazard analysis; the coupling is now deliberate and enforced, not accidental.
- **§5.2 streamed DATA + terminal share ONE ordered writer conduit; the priority-split second channel is deferred (M1-S5 Task 4a)** — the S3 `writer.rs`/`session/mod.rs` doc comments anticipated a *second, credit-limited data channel with control prioritized over data* for streamed result frames. As shipped (M1-S5 Task 4a), streamed HEAD/DATA frames and the request's terminal `END` instead share the SINGLE existing control channel, whose item became `ControlMsg { frame: OutFrame, cap: Option<CapReserve> }` (an `OutFrame` plus the optional per-session cap guard the writer drops after the write). This is what makes the streamed-request ordering invariant (a request's terminal never overtakes its own DATA) hold for free: DATA is enqueued during the handler run, the terminal only after (via the supervisor's already-reserved permit), so FIFO on the one channel puts the terminal last — no second channel and no cross-channel priority logic needed. The priority-split two-channel design is **DEFERRED** (charter rule 5 — no speculative throughput work before the gate); `writer::run`'s `tokio::select!` loop shape is retained so it can be reintroduced later without a rewrite. The per-session aggregate cap (`SessionCap`, §5.2) is released exactly once, when the writer drops the `ControlMsg` after the frame is written — so reserved bytes bound the bytes actually buffered toward the socket, not merely the enqueued ones.
- **§9.2/§19.3 MySQL statement-timeout errno DIVERGES between MySQL and MariaDB; the fate map keys on `errno`, not SQLSTATE (M1-S6 Task 4)** — the MySQL backend's `error_map` (`engine/crates/ferro-backend-mysql/src/error_map.rs`) classifies a `mysql_async::ServerError` by its **`errno`**, not its five-character SQLSTATE, because MySQL's SQLSTATE is too coarse to carry the §9.2 branches: a deadlock (`1213`) is SQLSTATE `40001` but a lock-wait timeout (`1205`) is the catch-all `HY000`, so a "class-40 ⇒ Retryable" heuristic (correct for PG) would silently miss `1205`. The **statement-timeout event has a different errno on each engine**: MySQL raises `3024` (`ER_QUERY_TIMEOUT`, from `MAX_EXECUTION_TIME`) while MariaDB raises `1969` (`ER_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT`, from `max_statement_time`); a `KILL QUERY` (this backend's out-of-band cancel) raises `1317` (`ER_QUERY_INTERRUPTED`) on both. All three are mapped to `errc::CANCELLED` so the **UNCHANGED** S4 `fate.rs::is_57014` override (which fires on `code == errc::CANCELLED`) yields the §19.3 cell (autocommit write → Indeterminate, in-tx → Retryable, read → Cancelled) with **no `fate.rs` edit** — the direct analog of PG's SQLSTATE `57014`. *[CLOSED by M1-S8a Task 3 — see (o) below: `PoolError::Sql` now carries an `errno: Option<i32>` slot, this module fills it from `se.code`, and `fate.rs` passes it to `ErrorPayload.errno` verbatim; the raw errno is now BOTH the classification key and a wire field.]* The raw SQLSTATE + server message are preserved verbatim on every arm. The T3-review fallback fix is also here: an unrecognized server error no longer maps to `errc::PROTOCOL` (reserved for a wire-protocol fault) but to a generic non-retryable SQL code (`errc::SYNTAX`, the broadest "server rejected this statement" NonRetryable bucket) — with a duplicate key (`1062`) mapped precisely to `errc::UNIQUE`.
- **§7/§20.1 the daemon pool registry is heterogeneous; `kind` is inferred from the DSN scheme; MySQL `fetch:stream` is deferred to M1-S7 (M1-S6 Task 5)** — *[SUPERSEDED by (n) below: MySQL streaming did NOT land in M1-S7 and is deferred out of M1-S8a as well; (n) records the structural cause, the ONE capability authority that now gates it, and the fact that the refusal is no longer restated per-arm]* — — `ferrod`'s `PoolRegistry` (`engine/crates/ferrod/src/pools.rs`) changed from a monomorphic `HashMap<String, Pool<PgBackend>>` to `HashMap<String, AnyPool>` where `AnyPool { Pg(Pool<PgBackend>), Mysql(Pool<MysqlBackend>) }`, so a `mysql://`/`mariadb://` DSN builds a `Pool<MysqlBackend>` and a `postgres://`/`postgresql://` DSN a `Pool<PgBackend>`. There is NO separate `kind =` config knob: `config::infer_pool_kind` reads the DSN scheme (the substring before `://`, case-insensitive; unknown/empty → Postgres default with a warn) and `PoolSpec` carries the resulting `PoolKind`. Dispatch is a two-arm `match AnyPool` at each `registry.get` site in `services::sql`, each arm calling ONE generic body — `run_exec_on_pool<B: PoolBackend>` (autocommit EXEC, extracted verbatim from the old inline `handle_exec` body) and `begin_on_pool<B>` (TX BEGIN, extracted from `handle_begin`); the tx actor (`actor::run<B>`) and `TxRegistry` (backend-agnostic command channels) were already generic, so the MySQL tx path spawns a `Checkout<MysqlBackend>` actor with no further change and PG's paths are byte-for-byte unchanged. Buffered `fetch:rows`/`none` run on MySQL; a `fetch:stream` EXEC to a MySQL pool is rejected EARLY (before checkout) with a clean `Unsupported` terminal ("fetch=stream is not yet supported on MySQL (lands in M1-S7 with the streaming bridge)" — the carry is now **M1-S8** after the S7 type slice was inserted) rather than the mid-stream `MysqlBackend::query_stream` `Unsupported` — MySQL streaming lands in M1-S8 (renumbered). Note: `actor::compose_begin_sql` used to emit PG-flavored `BEGIN [ISOLATION LEVEL …] [READ ONLY]` unconditionally, so a MySQL transaction requesting an explicit isolation or `readonly` failed at BEGIN with `ERROR 1064`; **that was CLOSED in M1-S8a Task 8** — the composer is dialect-aware and the MySQL family gets `START TRANSACTION [READ ONLY]` with the isolation as a same-statement `SET TRANSACTION …;` prefix. See (s) below.
- **§7.1/§7.2/§8 tracker-verification HARD GATE passed live on both engines; the tracker-clean hygiene skip stays DEFERRED (`clean_reset_profile()=Some(Full)`); a MySQL/MariaDB read-back divergence documented (M1-S6 Task 7)** — the §7.1 verification test (required in M1) is DONE: an acceptance gate (`engine/crates/ferro-backend-mysql/tests/pin_hygiene_it.rs`, live against MySQL 8.4.11 + MariaDB 11.8.8; `ferrod`'s `mysql_it.rs` is the daemon e2e) drives a real `Pool<MysqlBackend>` (`max_size=1`, forcing reuse) through `CALL p_set_session()` (a `SET SESSION sort_buffer_size` buried in a stored program, invisible to the assist lexer) and proves BOTH (a) **DETECTION** — the connection ends up `tainted` on both engines — and (b) **HYGIENE** — the next lease on the SAME physical conn (verified via the driver's protocol `id()`, NOT `CONNECTION_ID()` whose `BIGINT UNSIGNED` result is the deferred unsigned-64 type) ran `COM_RESET_CONNECTION` and reads the session variable back at the server default. **(a) The split-signal authority as shipped (§7.1):** the tx-info status flag drives `tx_status` (never `Failed`) and the OK-packet trackers drive `take_session_mutated`; both fire for the in-proc `SET` on both engines (already proven at the backend level in T3's `conn_it.rs`). At the `Checkout` pin-cause layer the CALL surfaces as `PinCause::Unknown` on both engines because `apply_classify` (the T6 unconditional `CALL`/`DO` backstop → `Unknown`) composes AFTER `apply_session_tracker` (→ `SessionTracker`) and "most recently observed" wins; the gate therefore accepts EITHER cause (both close the leak). A live consequence worth recording: the Checkout-level `SessionTracker` LABEL is effectively unobservable for real MySQL statements, because every session-mutating statement is ALSO lexer-classifiable (`SET`→`Set`, `CALL`/`DO`→`Unknown`, `LOCK TABLES`→`AdvisoryLock`) and the lexer's more-specific cause overwrites it — the tracker's load-bearing output is the *taint* (reuse-safety), not its label; the label authority is proven via the raw-backend `take_session_mutated` (which the parity test asserts directly for a direct `SET`). **(b) `clean_reset_profile()` returns `Some(Full)` — the tracker-clean `None`-skip is NOT enabled (R2, §7.2):** every recycled MySQL conn runs the full `COM_RESET_CONNECTION` regardless of taint, so the read-back cannot be a leak *discriminator* (the reset always runs); the gate's job is DETECTION + reset-efficacy. Enabling the skip is deferred until the tracker's in-proc coverage across all reuse-relevant session variables is proven — correctness over the §16 cache target. **(c) MySQL/MariaDB read-back divergence:** `p_set_session()` sets `sort_buffer_size = 262144`, which on **MySQL 8.4** is EXACTLY the server default (`@@global.sort_buffer_size = 262144`, verified live) — so the in-proc value is indistinguishable from the default and a bare read-back would be vacuous — whereas on **MariaDB 11.8** the default is `2097152`, so the in-proc `262144` is directly discriminating. To make the hygiene proof non-vacuous on both, the gate additionally installs a guaranteed-non-default probe (`default * 2`, a valid block-size multiple) on `co1` and asserts the next lease reads the default, never the probe. Both engines: `co1 tainted (cause=Unknown)`; `co2 sort_buffer_size == default` (MySQL 262144 with probe 524288 cleared; MariaDB 2097152 with probe 4194304 cleared) — leak CLOSED. No tracker gap observed on either engine (the in-proc `SET` taints via BOTH the OK-packet tracker and the `CALL` backstop on both); the `CALL`/`DO` lexer backstop is nonetheless retained as the belt for any future engine/version where the tracker might miss an in-proc mutation. This task is TEST + spec only — no backend/PG internals changed.
- **§9.1 MySQL scoped type policy recorded (M1-S6 Task 8; the scope was WIDENED by M1-S7 — see the slice-wide S7 entry below, which closed the `BIGINT UNSIGNED` / `DECIMAL` / date-time / `JSON` deferrals this entry records)** — §9.1 now documents the M1 MySQL backend's shipped scalar policy (`engine/crates/ferro-backend-mysql/src/rowmap.rs`): only `NULL/BOOL/I64/F64/TEXT/BYTES` are hydrated (keyed off column metadata: type + flags + charset + display length); every other column type is a **loud `PoolError::Unsupported`** naming the column + type, so a deferred type is a diagnosable error rather than a silent miscast. Deferred (each pending its §9.1 policy — `u64_overflow`/`decimal`/`naive_datetime_zone` — in a later slice): `BIGINT UNSIGNED`/all unsigned integers, `DECIMAL`/`NEWDECIMAL`, the `DATE`/`DATETIME`/`TIMESTAMP`/`TIME`/`YEAR` family, plus `JSON`/`BIT`/`ENUM`/`SET`/`GEOMETRY`/`VECTOR`. `TINYINT(1)` (display length 1, MySQL's `BOOLEAN` idiom) → `Bool`; wider signed integers → `I64`. This documents what the S8/S9 DBAL/Eloquent suite inherits (§14–15). The classifier itself shipped in T4; this task only records the pinned policy in the spec — no code changed.
